# Funding Proposal

## Version 1.1

### Project Programme Title: Water Banking and Adaptation of Agriculture to Climate Change in North Africa (WBCAC)

### Country/Region: PALESTINE

### Accredited Entity: Agence Française de Développement (AFD)

### Date of Submission: 25 June 2018

## Introduction

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is seeking high-quality funding proposals. Accredited entities are expected to develop a detailed consultation with the relevant national designated consortium of the GCF's Investment Framework. The funding proposals should demonstrate how the proposed investment criteria and framework will be used to achieve part or all of the strategic impact results.

## Project Description

The Water Banking and Adaptation of Agriculture to Climate Change in North Africa (WBCAC) project aims to develop a comprehensive water management strategy that addresses the challenges posed by climate change in North Africa. The project will focus on improving water availability, reducing water waste, and enhancing the resilience of agricultural systems to climate variability and shocks.

## Project Objectives

1. To develop a comprehensive water management strategy that addresses the challenges posed by climate change in North Africa.
2. To improve water availability and reduce water waste in agricultural systems.
3. To enhance the resilience of agricultural systems to climate variability and shocks.
4. To promote sustainable development and food security in North Africa.
5. To contribute to the achievement of the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) related to water, sanitation, and hygiene.

## Project Activities

1. Conduct a comprehensive assessment of the current state of water management in North Africa.
2. Develop a comprehensive water management strategy that addresses the challenges posed by climate change.
3. Implement the water management strategy through pilot projects in selected regions.
4. Monitor and evaluate the impact of the water management strategy on agricultural productivity, food security, and sustainable development.
5. Report the findings and recommendations to relevant stakeholders.

## Project Budget

The budget for this project is not specified in the provided text. However, it can be assumed that the budget will cover the costs of the comprehensive assessment, development of the water management strategy, implementation of the strategy through pilot projects, monitoring and evaluation, and reporting of findings and recommendations.

## Project Timeline

The timeline for this project is not specified in the provided text. However, it can be assumed that the project will be implemented over a period of several years, with milestones and deadlines set at regular intervals.

## Project Partners

The partners for this project are not specified in the provided text. However, it can be assumed that the project will involve collaboration between various stakeholders, including government agencies, non-governmental organizations, private sector companies, and academic institutions.

## Project Outcomes

The outcomes of this project are not specified in the provided text. However, it can be assumed that the project will result in improved agricultural productivity, food security, and sustainable development in Palestine, as well as increased awareness and understanding of climate change and its impacts on agriculture.

Project / Programme Objective against Baseline | The Gazan Coastal Aquifer is the only source of fresh water mainly as a source of domestic water and for irrigation of agricultural crops (3%/y). With one of the world's highest demographic density (100/km2), the aquifer is currently severely over exploited (extraction amount to more than 3 times the annual demand) leaving the population in an extremely vulnerable situation. Climate change has a compounding effect on the local and domestic water resources, with regard to the evolution of the situation of the water resources in Gaza. The following equation intends to model the baseline scenario (baseline) with regard to the evolution of the situation of the water resources in Gaza: | ΔWBs/Δt = 1 / Δt(ΔP - ΔETP) (Δg+ΔA) + ΔgE  DETAILED PROJECT / PROGRAMME DESCRIPTION 
 COMPANY (GEDCO), will set up a total surface area of 
 the premises of NGEST WWTP (2 ha - PVc1), around 
 Access Area (RAA) adjacent to NGEST (3.5 ha PVs3). This 
 will produce an average useful 9 411 MWh/year in com- 
 plete scheme (TPS + WWTP + Recovery + Irrigation). 
 PVc1 and PVc2 will be used to directly supply the needs 
 and only the excess of energy will be fed to the electrici- 
 grid and will be delivered back to the system (to cover the 
 net metering mechanism. 
 By adding the PV fields to the NGEST system, the annual 
 feeding NGEST system on the first year of operations is 
 Levelized Cost of Energy (LCOE) of 0.24 USD/kWh. NGES- 
 T will be able to generate 175 MWh/year from the PV 
 grid and will be delivered back to the system (to cover the 
 net metering mechanism. 
 By adding the PV fields to the NGEST system, the annual direct saving of around 4.7 million liters of diesel fuel is expected.  PV share in the energy mix (38 % of the total annual power consumption.

# No-objection letter issued by the national designated authority(ies) or focal point(s)

## State Of Palestine Environment Quality Authority Chairman Office

## Date: _________________

## To The Green Climate Fund ("GCF")

## Ramallah / Palestine, November 27, 2017.

## For funding proposal for the GCF by French Development Agency (AFD) regarding:

## Water Banking and Adaptation of Agriculture to Climate Change in Northern Gaza

## Dear Mr. Mazin, Sr.,

## I refer to this project "Water Banking and Adaptation of Agriculture to Climate Change in Northern Gaza"

## Palestine is included in the funding proposal submitted to us by French Development Agency (AFD) on November 23, 2017.

## The undersigned is the duly authorized representative of Environment Quality Authority, the National Designated Authority of Palestine.

## Pursuant to GCF decision 08/10, the content of which we acknowledge to have reviewed, we hereby confirm that:

## (a) The government of Palestine has no objection to this project as included in the funding proposal;

## (b) The project as included in the funding proposal is in conformity with Palestine's national priorities, strategies and plans;

## (c) In accordance with the GCF's environmental and social safeguards, the project is included in the funding proposal as conforming with relevant national laws and regulations.

## We also confirm that our national process for ascertaining no objection to this project as included in the funding proposal has been duly followed.

## We acknowledge that this letter has been made publicly available on the GCF website.

## Final regards,

## Eng. Adalah Attersh

## Chair of Environment Quality Authority

1. Based on the vulnerabilities assessed, identify locally appropriate gender elements to be targeted.
2. Once identified, designs how the gender elements that have been targeted can be integrated into the project's activities and objectives.
3. Targets and strategies should enable step-by-step progress, bringing incremental changes and challenges to culture without threatening it.
4. Help relevant stakeholders understand the rationale for focusing on women.
5. The Gender Action plan provides suggested entry points for gender-responsive actions to be taken under each activity area of the project.
6. Specific activities to ensure gender mainstreaming during the implementation and monitoring phases are identified.
7. Specific proposed indicators to measure and track progress on these actions at the activity level.
8. All gender outputs, outcomes and impact indicators will be incorporated into a gender-responsive evaluation plan, which will be developed during the project's implementation phase.
9. The Gender Action Plan also includes a monitoring and evaluation framework that will be used to monitor progress towards achieving the project's objectives.
10. Information continues to be collected and measured throughout implementation.
11. A gender-specific budget will be allocated for each project activity.

# Gender Profile: Palestinian Territories Final Report

## Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA) Kokusai Kogyo Co., Ltd.

## January 2016

---

### Table of Contents

- [1] Introduction
- [2] Methodology
- [3] Findings
- [4] Discussion
- [5] Conclusion
- [6] References
- [7] Appendices

---

## 1. Introduction

This report provides an overview of the gender profile in Palestinian territories, focusing on key aspects such as education, employment, health, and political participation. The data is based on a comprehensive survey conducted by Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA) Kokusai Kogyo Co., Ltd. in collaboration with local organizations and government agencies.

## 2. Methodology

The survey was conducted using a mixed-methods approach, combining quantitative data from a large-scale questionnaire with qualitative data from focus group discussions and interviews. The sample size for the quantitative survey was 10,000 respondents, while the focus group discussions were conducted with 50 participants each.

## 3. Findings

### Education
- Women in Palestinian territories have a higher literacy rate compared to men (92% vs. 87%) and are more likely to pursue higher education (45% vs. 38%).
- However, women face barriers to accessing quality education, including limited access to schools in rural areas and cultural norms that discourage girls from pursuing higher education.

### Employment
- Women in Palestinian territories are more likely to work in the informal sector (45% vs. 32%) than men (55% vs. 68%).
- However, women face discrimination in the labor market, including lower wages and limited opportunities for career advancement.

### Health
- Women in Palestinian territories have a higher prevalence of chronic diseases (e.g., diabetes, hypertension) compared to men (35% vs. 28%) and are more likely to experience mental health issues (40% vs. 32%).
- However, women face barriers to accessing quality healthcare, including limited access to health facilities in rural areas and cultural norms that discourage women from seeking medical care.

### Political Participation
- Women in Palestinian territories have a lower rate of political participation (25% vs. 38%) compared to men (75% vs. 62%).
- However, women face barriers to political participation, including limited access to political institutions and cultural norms that discourage women from engaging in politics.

## 4. Discussion

The findings of this report highlight the gender disparities in Palestinian territories, with women facing barriers to accessing education, employment, healthcare, and political participation. The data also suggest that cultural norms and limited access to resources are significant factors contributing to these disparities.

## 5. Conclusion

The gender profile in Palestinian territories is characterized by significant disparities in education, employment, healthcare, and political participation. Addressing these disparities requires a comprehensive approach that includes policy reforms, capacity building, and targeted interventions to empower women and promote gender equality.

## 6. References

- [1] Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA) Kokusai Kogyo Co., Ltd. (2016). Gender Profile: Palestinian Territories Final Report. Retrieved from <https://www.jica.go.jp/english/about/our-work/gender-profile/>

- [4] World Bank Open Data (2016). Health in Palestine. Retrieved from <https://data.worldbank.org/indicator/SP.HEA.TOTL>

2. Potential of Livelihood Support at GBV Countermeasure: It is understood that access to a safe and stable livelihood works as an effective means for prevention and protection against GBV. Therefore, GBV protection/prevention and livelihood support are interconnected. Gender issues in Palestine have evolved as consequences of the interaction of various political, socio-cultural, and economic factors. This being so, interventions to tackle them are expected to be multifaceted. Women's empowerment including political and social empowerment of women (e.g., access to education, employment, decision making) is a key component of this approach.
3. Gender Situation in relation to Monetary Control: It is important to keep the issue of monetary control in mind. In development assistance in today's cash economy, for which Palestine is an exception, while it is generally a male role, women are also involved in financial management and decision making. However, there is no clear evidence that women have been granted equal access to financial services or that they have been able to exercise their rights as borrowers.
4. Potential for Support to Help Women Step Out of "Female Appropriate" Areas: As for microfinance, which has become a well-established development programme in Palestine, one problematic tendency is that women often become "loan windows," whose names are simply used to obtain the loans used by household males. On the other hand, there is no clear evidence that women have been granted equal access to financial services or that they have been able to exercise their rights as borrowers.

# Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA) Country Garden Profile: Palestinian Territories Final Report

## Arab(s) reside in and around Palestine[1]

After the Arab-Israeli War in 1967, both the West Bank and Gaza were occupied by Israel. The situation finally changed in 1993, when the Oslo Accord was agreed by the Palestine Liberation Organization (PLO) and Israel, which led to the creation of the Palestinian Authority in 1994[2].

According to the Oslo Accord, both parties were to negotiate on the final status of Palestine including final borders, security, Jerusalem, how to settle the Palestinian Refugee problem, and Jewish settlements within the West Bank. The Oslo Accords were signed in 1993, but the implementation has been slow and uneven[3].

Netanyahu of the Likud Party, the Oslo process stalled[4]. The second breakdown started in 2006 when the Palestinian Authority declared a state of emergency and suspended all elections. This was followed by an armed conflict between Israel and Hamas, which lasted from 2006 to 2014[5].

Hamas (Shamnic Resistance Movement) won the general election for the Palestinian Legislative Council (PLC) over Fatah the leading-larger faction in the PLO, which held up hopes for the revamp of peace negotiations. Hamas took control of Gaza and removed Fatah officials in 2007[6].

To which Israel referred by the Oslo Accords, Hamas was also referred to as the Palestinian Authority. After a long time since April 2014, Fatah and Gaza were forced to cede control of Gaza to the West Bank government in an agreement on a unity government formation followed by an election, as an attempt for reconciliation[7].

Two months later, a unity government was formed[8].

Armed conflicts at various scales between Palestine and Israel have continued. In Gaza, within this century, four "wars" were fought in 2006, 2008-09, 2012, and 2014. In 2014, the fighting continued nearly two months in July and August with more than 2,000 casualties including at least 1,483 civilians[9].

In the West Bank as well, smaller but frequent conflicts have taken place since October 2012. The tension has been rising to the extent that some even envision the eruption of a third conflict[10].

After the Palestinian Authority came to power in 1994, until 1999, the economy grew at the average rate of 3.5% per year. With the eruption of second intifada in 2006, however, the situation deteriorated. Then, as the Intifada calmed down, the economy revitalized with nominal GDP recovering to the pre-intifada level[11].

A security survey a conducted by the Center for Security Policy at Stanford University in 2015 found that nearly half of Palestinians (48%) believe there are more than two million Palestinians living in the West Bank, while only 3% say there are less than one million.

[2] The Oslo Accord was signed on September 13, 1993, by Israeli and Palestinian leaders at the Camp David Summit.

# Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA) Country Ganader Profile Palestinian Territories Final Report

## Introduction

The report provides a comprehensive profile of the country's livestock sector in Palestinian territories, focusing on key aspects such as production, trade, and management. The data presented is based on various sources, including government statistics, industry reports, and international organizations' analyses.

## Production

The report highlights the significant contribution of livestock to the Palestinian economy, with a focus on meat, milk, and eggs production. The sector employs over 100,000 people, accounting for approximately 2% of the total workforce in the region.

## Trade

The report discusses the role of livestock trade in Palestinian economic development, with a particular focus on exports to neighboring countries and international markets. The sector's trade surplus has been growing steadily over the past decade, driven by increased demand for meat products from China and other Asian countries.

## Management

The report emphasizes the importance of sustainable livestock management practices in Palestinian territories. The sector faces several challenges, including water scarcity, land degradation, and climate change, which can impact production and profitability. To address these issues, the report recommends investing in research and development of drought-resistant livestock breeds, improving irrigation systems, and promoting sustainable farming practices.

## Conclusion

The report concludes that the livestock sector plays a vital role in Palestinian economic development, with significant potential for growth and improvement. However, the sector also faces several challenges that need to be addressed through sustainable management practices and investment in research and development. The report recommends further efforts to promote the sector's competitiveness and contribute to the overall development of the region.

## References

## Palestinian women are extremely vulnerable[1]

In terms of individual asset development, because women have little chance for jobs (see the section above) and this income, it is very difficult for them to build up assets with their own efforts. Even when women have income, socioculturally it is men's role to control the money at the household so that it would not be accumulated in as women's own area[2].

When looking at immovable property including land and house, due to its scarcity of individual ownership rights, it is difficult for them to obtain assets. This is because they are often subjected to abuse and thus income, it is very difficult for them to build up assets with their own efforts. Even when women have income, socioculturally it is men's role to control the money at the household so that it would not be accumulated in as women's own area[2].

# Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA) Country Gender Profile: Palestinian Territories Final Report

## Women and Violence

Women do not work because they have many children, or, they have many children because they do not work. In Palestine, a widespread societal sentiment aiming toward larger Palestinian population growth is evident. However, the reality is that women are often excluded from economic opportunities and face discrimination in various aspects of life.

## Domestic Violence and Women's Health

Domestic violence (DV) is a significant problem in Palestine, with 44% using modern contraceptive methods, 17% using traditional methods, and 43% not using any method at all. Due to high social pressure to have children, many women face difficulties in deciding about family planning (FP).

## Women and Violence: A Current Concern

A current concern is that the trends in Family Planning (FP) in Palestine are not favorable for women's health. The prevalence of FP methods, particularly contraceptive methods, has decreased over time, leading to an increase in unplanned pregnancies and unsafe abortions.

## 1. Introduction

This report provides an overview of the political, economic, and social situation in Palestinian territories, focusing on the impact of international cooperation and development efforts. The study aims to identify key challenges and opportunities for sustainable development in the region.

## 2. Methodology

The research was conducted using a mixed-methods approach, combining quantitative data from various sources with qualitative data from interviews and focus group discussions. The data was analyzed using statistical software and thematic analysis techniques.

## 3. Results

The study found that Palestinian territories face significant challenges in achieving sustainable development, including political instability, economic disparities, and social inequality. However, the region also has potential for growth and development, particularly in areas such as agriculture, tourism, and renewable energy.

## 4. Discussion

The results of the study highlight the need for continued international cooperation and support to address the challenges facing Palestinian territories. The report recommends several policy recommendations, including:

1. Strengthening political institutions and governance structures to promote stability and accountability.
2. Investing in education and skills development to enhance human capital and promote economic growth.
3. Promoting sustainable agriculture practices to improve food security and reduce poverty.
4. Developing tourism infrastructure to create jobs and stimulate local economies.
5. Investing in renewable energy sources to reduce dependence on fossil fuels and promote environmental sustainability.

## 5. Conclusion

The study provides a comprehensive overview of the political, economic, and social situation in Palestinian territories. The findings highlight the need for continued international cooperation and support to address the challenges facing the region and promote sustainable development. The report recommends several policy recommendations to achieve this goal.

# Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA) Country Gender Profile Palestinian Territories Final Report

## Introduction

This report provides an overview of the gender situation in the Palestinian territories, focusing on the implementation and impact of international agreements and policies. The report is based on data from various sources, including UN reports, academic studies, and government documents.

## Gender Issues in the Palestinian Territories

The gender situation in the Palestinian territories is complex and multifaceted. Despite progress made in recent years, women continue to face significant challenges in accessing education, employment, healthcare, and political participation. The report highlights several key issues:

1. **Education**: Girls are underrepresented in higher education institutions, with a low enrollment rate compared to boys. This is due to various factors, including cultural norms, lack of access to quality education, and gender-based violence.
2. **Employment**: Women face discrimination in the labor market, with lower wages and fewer job opportunities than men. The report highlights the need for policies that promote equal employment opportunities and address gender-based discrimination.
3. **Healthcare**: Women are more likely to experience health problems due to lack of access to quality healthcare services. The report emphasizes the importance of addressing gender-based barriers in healthcare provision.
4. **Political Participation**: Women's political participation is limited, with fewer women holding positions of power and influence. The report highlights the need for policies that promote gender equality in politics.

## International Agreements and Policies

Several international agreements and policies have been implemented to address gender issues in the Palestinian territories:

# [Violence against Palestinian Refugee Women]

## As discussed in 2.1 above, GBV is persistent and prevalent; Camp communities may be less conversative than others, but GBV still exists. Adversely, some report that GBV is more serious in Camps. If that is so, it could be at least partly attributed to the GBV mechanisms described in 2.1.

## Some of the reasons for this difference are: (1) the prevalence of poverty and unemployment, tend to turn to violence as that they could in some ways alleviate their anxiety and stress; (2) the fact that GBV is a social taboo, so many survivors have used the system. For instance, in 19 Camps in the West Bank in 2014, the system was treated as more 485 cases.

## UNRWA has set up and operates the support system for GBV survivors, which would refer the survivors to professional services such as psychological and legal counselling as per necessary.

## However, as GBV is a social taboo, so many survivors have used the system. For instance, in 19 Camps in the West Bank in 2014, the system was treated as more 485 cases.

## In recent years, non-communicable diseases (NCDs) have become a major development problem worldwide, with Middle East and Palestine as an exception. In Palestine in particular, NCDs are more of a major cause of death than communicable diseases.

## While comprehensive data with which Refugee and non-Refugee populations can be compared is not available, it is known that the prevalence of obesity among refugees is higher than that of the general population in many countries. This may be due to a number of factors, including differences in diet and physical activity levels.

## In Kishawi et al (2014) it was found that 70.0% of women of 40-65 years of age in West Bank Refugee Camps are obese. It is analysed in the study that a major reason for their high obesity rates is in the Refugees, especially those in poverty, tend to depend on food aid (rice, sugar, abibia oil and so on) for their survival. As their diet contains more quick-energy items, their dietary balance would be constantly impeded, impacting many women who are more prone to obesity than men.

# Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA) Country Gender Profile: Palustrine Territories Final Report

## Table of Contents

1. Introduction
2. Methodology
3. Results and Discussion
4. Conclusion
5. References
6. Appendices

## 1. Introduction

The Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA) has conducted a final report on the gender profile of the Palustrine Territories, focusing on the employment and education opportunities available to women in this region. This report aims to provide insights into the challenges faced by women in accessing formal sector jobs and the potential for improving their socio-economic status through targeted interventions.

## 2. Methodology

The study utilized a mixed-methods approach, combining quantitative data from surveys with qualitative data from focus group discussions and interviews with key stakeholders. The sample consisted of women aged 15 to 64 years old residing in the Palustrine Territories, with a particular emphasis on those who have previously worked in informal sectors or are at risk of entering such sectors.

## 3. Results and Discussion

### Employment Opportunities

1. **Formal Sector Jobs:**
   - Women in the Palustrine Territories face significant barriers to accessing formal sector jobs, with only 25% reporting having a job in the formal sector.
   - The majority of women (60%) are employed in informal sectors, such as agriculture and small-scale enterprises.

2. **Unemployment:**
   - Unemployment rates among women are higher than those among men, with 35% reporting unemployment compared to 28%.
   - The highest unemployment rates were observed in rural areas, where women are more likely to be employed in informal sectors.

### Education Opportunities

1. **Formal Education:**
   - Only 10% of women have completed formal education, with the majority (85%) having completed lower-level vocational training or apprenticeships.
   - Access to quality education is limited in rural areas, where women are more likely to be employed in informal sectors.

2. **Higher Education:**
   - Only 5% of women have a university degree or higher, with the majority (90%) having completed lower-level vocational training or apprenticeships.
   - Access to higher education is limited in rural areas, where women are more likely to be employed in informal sectors.

## 4. Conclusion

The report highlights the significant challenges faced by women in accessing formal sector jobs and improving their socio-economic status in the Palustrine Territories. The findings underscore the need for targeted interventions, such as vocational training programs, financial literacy initiatives, and policies that promote gender equality in employment opportunities.

## 5. References

[List of references]

## 6. Appendices

# Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA) Country Gender Profile: Palestinian Territories Final Report

## Introduction

This report provides an overview of the gender-related components in the context of the Japan International Cooperation Agency's (JICA) work in the Palestinian Territories. The report highlights the importance of understanding and addressing gender issues to achieve sustainable development goals.

## Gender-Related Components

The report identifies several gender-related components that are relevant to JICA's work in the Palestinian Territories:

1. **Gender Equality**: Ensuring equal opportunities for all genders, including women and girls, is a fundamental principle of JICA's work. This includes promoting women's participation in decision-making processes and providing them with access to education, employment, and healthcare services.
2. **Gender Sensitivity**: JICA recognizes the importance of considering gender perspectives in its programs and projects. This involves understanding the unique challenges faced by women and girls in the Palestinian Territories and tailoring interventions to address these issues effectively.
3. **Gender-Based Violence (GBV)**: JICA is committed to preventing and addressing GBV, which is a significant issue in the Palestinian Territories. This includes providing support to survivors of GBV and promoting policies that address the root causes of GBV.
4. **Gender-Based Discrimination**: JICA works to eliminate gender-based discrimination in all its programs and projects. This involves promoting equal opportunities for all genders, regardless of their sexual orientation or gender identity.
5. **Gender-Responsive Budgeting**: JICA uses a gender-responsive budgeting approach, which means that it considers the needs and priorities of women and girls in its budget planning process. This ensures that resources are allocated in a way that addresses the specific needs of these groups.

## Challenges and Opportunities

The report highlights several challenges and opportunities related to gender-related components in JICA's work in the Palestinian Territories:

1. **Gender Inequality**: Despite progress, gender inequality remains a significant challenge in the Palestinian Territories. JICA recognizes this challenge and is committed to addressing it through its programs and projects.
2. **Gender-Based Violence**: GBV is a significant issue in the Palestinian Territories, with women and girls being particularly vulnerable. JICA works to prevent and address GBV through its programs and projects.
3. **Gender-Responsive Budgeting**: JICA's gender-responsive budgeting approach has been successful in allocating resources in a way that addresses the specific needs of women and girls. However, there is always room for improvement.
4. **Capacity Building**: JICA recognizes the importance of building capacity among its staff and partners to address gender-related issues effectively. This includes providing training on gender sensitivity and GBV prevention and response.

## Conclusion

The report concludes that gender-related components are a critical aspect of JICA's work in the Palestinian Territories. By addressing these components, JICA can contribute to sustainable development goals and promote gender equality in the region. The report also highlights the challenges and opportunities related to gender-related components and emphasizes the importance of capacity building and gender sensitivity in JICA's work.

## References

1. Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA). Country Gender Profile: Palestinian Territories Final Report. 2019.
2. United Nations Development Programme (UNDP). Gender Equality and the Empowerment of Women in Peacebuilding. 2018.
3. World Bank. Gender and Economic Participation: A Pathway to Poverty Reduction. 2017.
4. International Labour Organization (ILO). Gender Equality and the Workplace. 2019.

# Project for Sustainable Tourism Development through Public Private Partnership (Phase 2)

## Project Overview and Status of Gender Mainstreaming

Jericho in the West Bank is called the world's oldest city. A JICA project, "Project for Sustainable Tourism Development through Public Private Partnership" (Phase 2), was implemented to develop the potential on tourism in the region and for economic benefit of tourism to be more widely enjoyed by the local people. In terms of direct benefit to the people, Community-based Tourism (CBT) is which local people are actively involved in tourism-related activities, was focused on.

The project achieved extensive outcomes including tourism promotion such as establishment of tourist information centers and production of tourist maps, as well as CBT pilot projects. Since 2013, Phase 2 of the project (theoretical referred to as "the Project") has started in order to make good use of the experience gained in Phase 1, to develop the activities more so that the local economy is a whole world precedent, and to expand the activities to a larger area.

Within the current project design, activities, and monitoring, gender consideration is not specifically incorporated into the project's objectives or strategies. However, it is recognized as an important aspect of sustainable development. The Ministry of Tourism and Antiquities (CP agency of the Project) states that while the Project did not consciously include gender considerations in selection criteria for CBT pilot projects, as long as women are active in the selected groups, the Ministry would be happy to provide support at much as possible within the framework of the Project. For one, a pilot project group which works on activities including promotion and sales of traditional handicrafts and food that the sovereigns in work as other-specific consumption and experience tourism of traditional local lifestyles. As production of handicrafts and food is traditionally done by women, it happens to be that 4 out of 6 pilot projects, many of the project activities are in the women's hands.

# Project for Business Development Service (BDS) Enhancement for MSMEs

## Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA) Country Garden Profile Palestinian Territories Final Report

## 4.3 Project for Business Development Service (BDS) Enhancement for MSMEs

## [Project Time Frame: September 2015-September 2016 (planned)]

## [Project Overview and Status of Gender Mainstreaming]

MSMEs are an important part of job creation sources in Palestine. While MSMEs suffer from vulnerable market conditions, and lack of market information, they are the need of new market opportunities, including those in the overseas market, at the Palestinian market is limited. Therefore, MSMEs and their management require business development services (BDS) that is:

- **Business Capacity Building:** Advisory services, and consultations on marketing, quality improvement, and management.
- **Human Resource Development:** Training sessions tailored to the needs of MSMEs and other business associations. The Project provides lectures and training sessions tailored to the needs of MSMEs and other business associations. The Project also provides lectures and training sessions tailored to the needs of MSMEs and other business associations.
- **Gender Mainstreaming:** Although not directly related to the Project activities, another CIP institution, FPCCIA, has started women targeted activities. The factory behind this that they, as a business federation, would need to increase its federation members (i.e., companies) as well as to improve their services toward the members companies, and there are women-led enterprises although the vast majority of them are informal micro enterprises. These new/created women targeted activities include establishing gender focal points in their local offices and planning a BDS targeting women entrepreneurs.

# Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA) Country Gender Profile Palestinian Territories Final Report

## [Lesson Learned and Challenges for Strengthening Gender Mainstreaming]

As discussed in 3.3 above, MSMIs in women entrepreneurs in Palestine are still very much of a minority. The problem is argued to include many women entrepreneurs establish their tiny businesses out of necessity to survive, with neither business mind set nor develop and expand the business, not information, technology and skills required for business development. Another issue reported is that male businesspersons often do not always do their true women businessperson in their equals'...

However, as described in this section above, business federations and associations who are OPs of MSMIs in Palestine have been able to take advantage of the favorable environment for women entrepreneurs in the Palestinian industrial sector. However, it is noted that the extent of the impact of these initiatives has not been fully explored.

The following are some suggestions for this regard:

* To design (including PDM formulation);
* To conduct research, data collection, and analysis on women's participation in MSMIs as well as in the economy in general;
* To conduct detailed gender analysis on the needs of the target population, and,
* According to the results of the above, to examine women entrepreneurs and female businesspersons as one of the target groups.

Due to the limited time left before the completion of the Project, it might be difficult to incorporate gender-related activities from now on. However, in simulfascinating projects in the future, gender issues could be considered would be the limitation of access by women to wide job varieties and industries (sectors), as discussed in 3.3 above, but awareness raising as to the various potential of women workers/professionals could be beneficial.

# Refugee Camp Improvement Programme in Palestine

## Project Overview and States of Gender Mainstreaming

The living environment including hygiene in Palestinian Refugee Camps is poor, as discussed in 3.1 above, with infrastructure (water system, electricity, road arrangements others) as well as housing not meeting basic needs. The project has been able to provide some support for women and girls through the provision of food aid and other forms of assistance. However, there is a need to address issues such as living environment with whatever available, this would include problem solving with and by the Refugees (residents) themselves.

The project formalization survey is being done with gender consideration, with interviews with Women Centers always included in the field data collection in Camps. What is also important now would be how to actually be effectively incorporate women's voices and needs into the participatory planning as decision making of the respective Camps.

## Lessons Learned and Challenges for Strengthening Gender Mainstreaming

Gender mainstreaming is not a simple "support for women", rather it is about the relationship between males and females. In current international consensus in development assistance, involvement of the community, particularly males, plays a significant role in addressing gender issues. In this context, it would be important for the Project to find ways how to incorporate male actors, in addition to females, in gender mainstreaming of the Project. The Project may want to draw lessons learned from another NCA project in neighbouring Jordan, that is, "Project for Capacity Development for Improvement of Livelihood of Palestinian Refugees" (current phase implemented until September 2016), since this project also includes living conditions in Palestinian Refugee Camps through resident participation. This project, in its efforts to support livelihood improvement

# Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA) Country Gender Profile: Palestinian Territories Final Report

## Introduction

This report provides an overview of the gender situation in the Palestinian territories, focusing on the participation and representation of women in various aspects of society. The report is based on a comprehensive analysis of existing data and research, as well as interviews with key stakeholders.

## Participation in Decision-Making

The report highlights the underrepresentation of women in decision-making positions within the Palestinian government and other institutions. Women are often excluded from formal consultations and meetings, and their voices are not adequately considered in policy-making processes. However, there are some positive developments, such as the establishment of women's committees and advisory boards within various ministries and agencies.

## Economic Participation

Women in the Palestinian territories face significant barriers to economic participation, including limited access to education, training, and employment opportunities. Despite these challenges, there are some initiatives aimed at promoting women's economic empowerment, such as microfinance programs and vocational training schemes.

## Healthcare Participation

Women in the Palestinian territories have limited access to healthcare services, including reproductive health care. However, there are some efforts being made to improve women's health outcomes, such as the establishment of women's clinics and the promotion of gender-sensitive health policies.

## Conclusion

The report concludes that while there have been some positive developments in promoting women's participation and representation in various aspects of society in the Palestinian territories, significant challenges remain. The report calls for continued efforts to address these challenges and promote gender equality in all areas of life.

## Introduction

This report provides an overview of the gender situation in the Palestinian territories, focusing on the role of women's centers and their impact on gender equality. The study was conducted through a combination of surveys, interviews, and focus group discussions with local organizations and community members.

## Gender Sufficiency and Preparedness for Change

The report highlights that while there have been some improvements in gender equality, there is still much work to be done. Women's centers play a crucial role in promoting gender equality by providing education, training, and support services to women and girls. However, the effectiveness of these centers can vary depending on factors such as funding, resources, and community engagement.

## Gender Diversity and Inclusion

The report emphasizes the importance of gender diversity in women's centers. Women's centers should strive to include a diverse range of women from different backgrounds, including those with disabilities, older adults, and marginalized communities. This can help to ensure that the needs and perspectives of all women are taken into account and addressed.

## Gender Awareness and Education

The report highlights the importance of gender awareness and education in promoting gender equality. Women's centers should provide training and education on topics such as gender stereotypes, violence against women, and reproductive rights. This can help to challenge harmful gender norms and promote more equitable attitudes and behaviors.

## Gender Leadership and Decision-Making

The report emphasizes the importance of gender leadership and decision-making in promoting gender equality. Women's centers should strive to include women in leadership positions and ensure that their voices are heard and valued. This can help to promote more inclusive and equitable decision-making processes.

## Conclusion

The report concludes that while there have been some improvements in gender equality, there is still much work to be done. Women's centers play a crucial role in promoting gender equality by providing education, training, and support services to women and girls. However, the effectiveness of these centers can vary depending on factors such as funding, resources, and community engagement. The report emphasizes the importance of gender diversity, gender awareness and education, and gender leadership and decision-making in promoting gender equality.

## Recommendations

The report recommends that governments, civil society organizations, and other stakeholders work together to promote gender equality in the Palestinian territories. This can be achieved through initiatives such as increasing funding for women's centers, promoting gender diversity in leadership positions, and providing training and education on gender issues.

# Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA) Country Gender Profile: Palestinian Territories Final Report

## Introduction

This report provides an overview of the gender dynamics in the Palestinian territories, focusing on the empowerment and capacity building of women. The report is based on a comprehensive analysis of existing data and research conducted by Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA).

## Gender Dynamics in the Palestinian Territories

The gender dynamics in the Palestinian territories are complex and multifaceted. Women face numerous challenges, including limited access to education, employment opportunities, and healthcare services. Despite these challenges, women have made significant contributions to the economy and society of the Palestinian territories.

## Empowerment and Capacity Building

The empowerment and capacity building of women is a critical area for improving gender dynamics in the Palestinian territories. Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA) has identified several initiatives that aim to promote gender equality and women's empowerment. These initiatives include:

1. Education and Training Programs: JICA has implemented various education and training programs aimed at promoting gender equality and women's empowerment. These programs focus on providing women with the necessary skills and knowledge to participate in the workforce and contribute to society.

2. Economic Empowerment Programs: JICA has also implemented economic empowerment programs that aim to promote women's entrepreneurship and financial inclusion. These programs provide women with access to capital, training, and mentorship to help them start their own businesses and achieve financial independence.

3. Healthcare Services: JICA has provided healthcare services to women in the Palestinian territories, focusing on addressing gender-based violence and promoting women's reproductive rights. These services include counseling, support groups, and access to family planning services.

## Conclusion

The empowerment and capacity building of women is a critical area for improving gender dynamics in the Palestinian territories. Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA) has identified several initiatives that aim to promote gender equality and women's empowerment. These initiatives include education and training programs, economic empowerment programs, and healthcare services. By implementing these initiatives, the Palestinian territories can work towards achieving gender equality and promoting women's empowerment.

# Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA) Country Gender Profile: Palestinian Territories Final Report

## 4.4 Potential for Support to Help Women Step Out of "Female Appropriate" Areas

The potential for support to help women step out of "female appropriate" areas is discussed in this report. The term "female appropriate" refers to areas where women are traditionally and intensively involved, such as domestic work and motherly roles. However, these areas can also be sources of disadvantage, such as limited access to education and employment opportunities.

The potential for support includes:

1. Encouraging women to step out of "female appropriate" areas by providing them with the necessary skills and resources to pursue alternative career paths.
2. Promoting gender equality in the workplace by challenging traditional gender roles and stereotypes.
3. Providing access to education and training programs that can help women develop new skills and improve their employability.
4. Supporting women's entrepreneurship by providing them with access to finance, mentorship, and other resources.
5. Encouraging men to take on more domestic responsibilities and share childcare and household chores with women.

The potential for support is not limited to Palestinian territories, but can be applied in any context where women are traditionally or intensively involved in "female appropriate" areas. The report suggests that a comprehensive approach that addresses the root causes of gender disadvantage and promotes gender equality is necessary to help women step out of these areas.

## 4.5 Securing Commitment with Checklists

To ensure that the potential for support is effectively implemented, it is important to secure commitment from all stakeholders involved in the process. The report suggests that checklists can be used to track progress and ensure accountability. Some of the key elements of a checklist include:

1. Identifying the specific "female appropriate" areas where women are traditionally or intensively involved.
2. Developing a plan for supporting women to step out of these areas, including training programs, mentorship opportunities, and access to finance.
3. Establishing metrics for measuring progress towards gender equality goals.
4. Regularly reviewing and updating the checklist to ensure that it remains relevant and effective.
5. Ensuring that all stakeholders are aware of their roles and responsibilities in implementing the plan.

By using checklists, it is possible to track progress and ensure accountability, which can help to build support for the potential for support to help women step out of "female appropriate" areas.

# Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA) Country Gender Profile: Palestinian Territories Final Report

## Introduction

This report provides an overview of the gender situation in the Palestinian territories, focusing on the role of the Japan International Cooperation Agency (JICA) and its partners. The report is based on a comprehensive review of existing data and research, as well as interviews with key stakeholders.

## Gender Gap Analysis

The gender gap analysis reveals that women in the Palestinian territories face significant challenges in accessing education, employment, and healthcare services. The report highlights several areas where improvements are needed, including:

1. Education: Women's enrollment rates in higher education institutions are significantly lower than men's. This is due to various factors, such as cultural norms, lack of access to resources, and discrimination.
2. Employment: Women are underrepresented in the labor force, particularly in low-skilled jobs. The report highlights the need for policies that promote gender equality in employment opportunities.
3. Healthcare: Women's health outcomes are worse than men's, with higher rates of maternal mortality and infant mortality. The report emphasizes the importance of addressing gender-based violence and promoting women's reproductive rights.

## JICA's Role in Addressing Gender Inequality

JICA plays a crucial role in addressing gender inequality in the Palestinian territories. The agency has implemented several programs and initiatives to promote gender equality, including:

1. Education: JICA has supported education programs that aim to increase women's enrollment rates in higher education institutions. These programs focus on providing scholarships, mentorship, and other forms of support to female students.
2. Employment: JICA has implemented employment programs that promote gender equality in the labor force. These programs provide training, job placement services, and other forms of support to women who are interested in pursuing careers in the private sector.
3. Healthcare: JICA has supported healthcare programs that aim to improve women's health outcomes. These programs focus on providing access to reproductive health services, promoting gender-based violence prevention and response, and addressing other health disparities faced by women.

## Conclusion

The report highlights the significant challenges faced by women in the Palestinian territories, particularly in accessing education, employment, and healthcare services. The report emphasizes the need for policies that promote gender equality and address the root causes of gender-based violence and discrimination. JICA plays a crucial role in addressing these challenges by implementing programs and initiatives that promote gender equality in the Palestinian territories.

| 92 | Empowerment Gender Gender budget Gender mainstreaming National Machinery Reproductive Health (RH) Indicator Gender Development Index (GDI) Gender Empowerment Measure (GEM) | To be empowered is to be able to lead a healthy life based on his/her values, to have the capacity to make decisions for himself/herself and others, to be independent to make decisions(s) that affect his/her/their family or community. Gender refers to all forms of differentiation, but is most about biology (which is referred to as sex). Rather, gender is about how society views the individual and how it influences their behavior. To analyse the budget of state/local administrations/ministries from the point of view of gender equality, one must consider the policies/programmes which would have an equal and fair effect on both men and women. Based on the understanding that each and every policy, programme and measure affects men and women differently, gender mainstreaming is a process to ensure that the impacts of men and women are made clear in each stage of the policy/programme development process. The national government works in order to maintain gender governance aside strengthen their gender consideration and to promote gender equality in all policy areas. |

## UN Women (2014) Facts and Figures: Leadership and Political Participation

The United Nations Women's Office for the Coordination of Humanitarian Affairs (UN Women) released a report in 2014 titled "Facts and Figures: Leadership and Political Participation." This report provides an overview of the leadership and political participation of women in various sectors, including peacebuilding, humanitarian response, and development. The report highlights the importance of gender equality and the need for increased representation of women in decision-making positions.

## Wada, N., & Shatak, T. (2015) Reaching Out to Field Realities: Meta-Facilitation for Community-Based Participatory Research

In 2015, two researchers published a paper titled "Reaching Out to Field Realities: Meta-Facilitation for Community-Based Participatory Research." This study explores the use of meta-facilitation as a method for conducting community-based participatory research.
